================================================================================
GAME BALANCE AUTHORITY — SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH + ENFORCED, TUNABLE SCHEMA
================================================================================
This document is the balance CONTRACT for toon3D — the replacement for the
old, deleted contract doc (balance-rule-system). It is the single source of truth for HOW
balance is governed; the factual map of the current balance STATE lives in
docs/game-balance-knowledge.txt (read that first for context and the known
problems). Created by new-game-balancr order 1; orders 2-9 register their rules
into the same schema.

CONTRACT: update this document in the SAME COMMIT as any change to
util/BalanceSchema.java, any new/changed band in util/BalanceConfig.java, or any
waiver. After any BalanceConfig change, re-run BalanceReport and paste its
output over SECTION 6 below.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 1 — THE THREE LAYERS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER 1 — VALUES (tunable):        util/BalanceConfig.java
  EVERY gameplay-affecting number lives here, organised into 17 numbered
  sections (1-14 as before, plus 15 WEAPON ABILITY MAGNITUDES and 16 SHOP
  ECONOMY, both consolidated by order 1, and 19 ROUTE ECONOMICS, added by
  order 7 — the branching MAP's numbers: node budget scales, affix multipliers,
  calm/elite/mystery/event payoffs, the audited map shape, the node-pricing
  model's knobs and every route band; shared status magnitudes joined
  SECTION 8, boss stats + boss AI tactic weights joined SECTION 14). Change a
  value -> the game gets easier/harder/more generous, and NOTHING else changes.
  The other *Constants files only RE-EXPORT these values under their old names
  (shims), so call sites never churned. EffectConstants now holds ONLY cosmetic
  values (colors, particle counts, screen shake); EnemyConstants keeps only
  cosmetic boss values (accent colors, sprites, telegraph visuals).

LAYER 2 — RULES (the schema):      util/BalanceSchema.java
  A declarative registry of every constraint the values must satisfy. Rules are
  DATA — registered subjects + bands evaluated through GameMath — never switch
  statements (the route/RouteRegistries discipline). A rule = (subject, formula
  via GameMath, band, waiver?). The schema also owns the content REGISTRIES the
  living tables print from: rangedWeapons(), telegraphAttacks(), healPickups(),
  scarcityRows(), the role-band maps, and the item classifications.

LAYER 3 — ENFORCEMENT:             core/src/test/.../util/BalanceAuditTest.java
  A plain JUnit test (pure JVM, no LibGDX) that runs every registered rule and
  FAILS THE BUILD on any unwaived violation. `./gradlew test` is the gatekeeper.
  One test method per rule kind, so a failure names the exact subject, value,
  and band. util/BalanceReport.java stays the human-readable table printer — a
  VIEW of the schema (its WEAPONS / ENEMIES / TELEGRAPH / WAIVERS tables iterate
  the schema registries), but the report can only WARN; the test GATES.

TUNABILITY IS PRESERVED BY CONSTRUCTION: designers tune LAYER 1 freely; the
schema only constrains values to their declared bands. A value that must
deliberately sit outside its band needs an explicit WAIVER (SECTION 4) —
visible, reasoned, auditable — never a silent exception.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 2 — THE FOUR PRIMITIVES + DERIVED SCORES (pointers, not re-derivations)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
All formulas live in util/GameMath.java as pure static methods with
Formula/Derivation/Edge-case blocks. Full walkthrough: knowledge doc SECTION 2.

  eHP  effectiveHitPoints(...)         (rawHP + armor) * dodge * flat-reduction terms
  DPT  sustainedDamagePerTurn(...)     (clip * dmg) / (clip + reloadTicks)
       ammoEfficiency(...)             damagePerShot / ammoPerShot
  TTK  turnsToKill(...)                ceil(target.eHP / attacker.DPT)
  TP   threatPoints(...)               enemyDPT * survivalTurns * positionalMult

  roleScenarioClear(...) = fractional turns to clear an R-ROLE scenario (balance-overhaul
                           order 3; replaced weaponPowerScore, deleted with R-WEAPON)
  goldenRatio(...)       = turnsToDie / turnsToKill (TTK at the SUSTAINED
                           reference DPT — contract decision)
  depthThreatScale(...)  = healthScale^(d-1) * damageScale^(d-1)
  playerPowerAtDepth(...)= 1 + budgetPP * levelsPerDepth * (d-1) / 100  (v1 cards-only)
  playerPowerAtDepthV2(..)= cardPower * gearRamp * abilityPower (HONEST, all sources; order 4)
  depthCouplingRatio(...)= playerPowerV2 / enemyThreat
  xpRequiredForLevelGeometric(..)= base * growth^(level-1)  (order 4)
  xpRewardAtDepth(...)   = round(xpPerThreatPoint * enemyThreatAtDepth)  (order 4)
  turnsToKillBreakpointGain(..)= TTK(before) - TTK(after)  (order 4)
  survivalTurnsBought(...)= healValue / averageIncomingDamagePerTurn

  REFERENCE ANCHORS (BalanceConfig SECTION 9): REFERENCE_PLAYER_DPT = 25,
  REFERENCE_PLAYER_EHP = 205, REFERENCE_AMMO_EFFICIENCY = 40. The eHP anchor is
  THE canonical start (SECTION 7 PLAYER_START_* block, 130 HP + 75 armour) — the
  game has exactly one difficulty, so it describes every real run (R-SINGLE-DIFFICULTY).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SECTION 3 — THE SCHEMA RULES (kind, band, and WHY the band is shaped so)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
R-ROLE (RuleKind.WEAPON_ROLE) — balance-overhaul order 3 — every weapon is the
best answer SOMEWHERE and never everywhere.
  The registry is util/WeaponRoleModel: one row per ranged weapon, whose
  DamageClass, ammo type, clip, reload, range and accuracy are read from a FRESH
  INSTANCE of the weapon (one source of truth), plus its scenario shape (hits and
  ammo per action, SINGLE / PIERCE / CONE / SPLASH / CHAIN, charge, burn stacks,
  knockback / stagger, self-damage). Eight REFERENCE SCENARIOS (archetype = the
  representative of the trait and role, BalanceConfig SECTION 22 geometry):
    S1 FLESH single 3-5 (Plague Hulk)       S5 BURNABLE x3 1-3 (Ghoul)
    S2 FLESH x3 1-3 (Gore Biter, the A6     S6 CHITIN single 1-2 (Shell Brute)
       "on-curve chaff")                    S7 INFERNAL single 3-5 (Void Shroud)
    S3 SHIELDED single 3-5 (Acid Drone)     S8 a BRUISER charging from 3 tiles
    S4 PLATED single 3-6 (Cinderforge          (Shell Brute) — scored in HITS it
       Colossus, the PLATED tank)              lands before it dies
  evaluated for the ON-CURVE player (GameMath.expectedPlayerAtDepth; hits scale
  by its ladder factor, enemies by the NEUTRAL eHP at the depth) at depths 5 and
  15. GameMath.roleScenarioClear walks the fight turn by turn (reload, Railgun
  charge, fire, then burn ticks) and reports FRACTIONAL turns to clear (the last
  turn counts only the share the killing blow needed), actions and ammo;
  GameMath.roleScenarioBruiserHitsTaken plays S8, crediting the SPREAD knockback
  (one tile) and stagger (one cancelled action, never two turns running).
  MODEL ASSUMPTIONS: expected-value accuracy on every hit; no crits, abilities or
  cards; a group is clustered in the band (a cone or splash reaches all of it, a
  pierce ROLE_GROUP_TARGETS_IN_LANE = 2 of it, the Arc's chain up to its jumps at
  never-resisted damage); the Railgun always fires at FULL charge (one charge
  turn, then the shot). "Best" = fewest turns among ammo-FEASIBLE clears (ties:
  every tied class is best).
    R-ROLE-1 NICHE      every ranged class but BALLISTIC is best in >= 1 scenario
                        by >= 1.20x over the best BALLISTIC weapon (S8: on hits+1).
                        Melee (BLADE / BLUNT) is EXEMPT: no ammo, adjacent only,
                        the fallback — not modelled (recorded decision).
    R-ROLE-2 GENERALIST BALLISTIC within 1.30x of the best in S1; never best in
                        S3 or S4.
    R-ROLE-3 NO DOMINANCE no class best in more than 2 scenarios (per depth).
    R-ROLE-4 RISK PAYS  every SPREAD weapon's SUSTAINED per-turn damage at 1-2
                        tiles (accuracy-weighted, reload counted) >= 2.0x the
                        Assault Rifle's at 3 tiles. The reference is the Assault
                        Rifle — W1's own wording and R-LADDER's generalist — not the
                        Chaingun (whose three-round action sustains ~2x the rifle).
    R-ROLE-5 AMMO FEASIBLE every class clears its DECLARED niche scenario
                        (ROLE_DECLARED_NICHE_BY_CLASS: BALLISTIC S1, SPREAD S6,
                        ENERGY S3, RAIL S4, FIRE S5, EXPLOSIVE S2 — reviewer fix:
                        it read the first scenario the class was closest to the best
                        in, which for RAIL was S1, so AS8's PLATED slug check never
                        ran) with <= one AVERAGE COMBAT floor's planned supply of its
                        ammo type (the CURRENT SupplyPlanner over 20 rosters, the
                        player carrying BULLETS + that type).
    A6                  the Incinerator clears S2 in <= 2 sprays at both depths.
  FITTED (CP3b): Shotgun 44 -> 66, Double-Barrel 32 -> 59 (0.9x per barrel,
  W2), Arc Cannon 28 -> 40, Railgun full charge 90 -> 75, Grenade centre/
  neighbour 42/22 -> 36/32, Incinerator burn fraction 0.5 -> 0.6 (impact 12 / 8
  kept), shell box 4 -> 3 and shell cap 17 -> 11 (same damage per box / banked).
  Assault Rifle and Chaingun unchanged; R-LADDER untouched. Resulting niches:
  RAIL S1 + S4, EXPLOSIVE S2, ENERGY S3 + S7, FIRE S5, SPREAD S6 + S8; BALLISTIC
  best nowhere, within 1.23x of S1's best.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (balance-overhaul order 3 OVERRIDE CLAUSE):
    R-WEAPON (RuleKind.WEAPON_POWER) — RETIRED. It required every ranged weapon's
    weaponPowerScore (sustained DPT x sqrt(ammo efficiency / 40)) to land in its
    role band: sidearm 8-14 | workhorse 12-18 | burst/specialist 18-26 |
    heavy/charge 24-32, with crediting notes (Railgun at full charge; Grenade on
    centre splash; Arc on the primary bolt; Incinerator as impact + one burn).
    It was blind to WHAT a weapon is good against — the reason every gun felt
    the same and the Chaingun (scored per bullet, not per three-round action)
    read as a mid-band workhorse. REPLACED by R-ROLE-1..5 above.
    WAIVER [WEAPON_POWER] Railgun (full charge) 45.0 vs heavy 24-32 — DELETED
    (AS8): the Railgun is governed by R-ROLE (the PLATED niche + R-ROLE-5).
    REFERENCE_AMMO_EFFICIENCY, the WEAPON_POWER_* bands and
    GameMath.weaponPowerScore are deleted with it.

R-ENEMY (RuleKind.ENEMY_THREAT_POINTS + ENEMY_HITS) — RE-BANDED by balance-overhaul order 1 (R8)
  Part 2 — THE R8 HIT BANDS (replace the golden-ratio bands): every non-boss
  archetype's depth-1 fight length lands in its role's targets, measured against
  the on-curve reference — the Assault Rifle, COMMON L1, at 3 tiles (15.2 per hit)
  — and the 205-eHP start player:
                 hits to kill   ordinary hits the player survives
    CHAFF          1-2            >= 10
    SOLDIER        3-4            7-9
    BRUISER        5-7            4-6
    MINI_ELITE     8-12           3-5
  (BalanceSchema.enemyHitResults / enemyHitsToKill / enemyHitsToDie). There are no
  exemptions any more: the chaff pack exemption and the mini-elite spike exemption
  of the golden ratio are gone — every role has a target.
  Part 1 — TP BANDS, RE-DERIVED from R8: each role's band is COMPUTED from the
  centre of its R8 box (centre eHP = mean hits-to-kill x 15.2; centre hit =
  205 / mean hits-to-die, an open-ended band using 1.5 x its floor; TP at melee
  positional, cadence 1) spread by LADDER_TP_BAND_LOW/HIGH_FACTOR (0.5 / 1.6) —
  wide enough for ranged/status positional (<= 1.55) and cadence-2 archetypes.
  WHY: the owner's "no more sponges": fights are short and hits land hard, and
  the TP currency stays priced against the same box the fight is.
  DEATH SPLASH TERM (sump-wretch-acid-death): an archetype whose death poisons its
  orthogonal neighbours (EnemyType.deathSplashPlayerStacks() > 0) adds
  GameMath.deathSplashThreatPoints(perStack, stacks, DEATH_SPLASH_ADJACENT_SHARE) =
  perStack x S(S+1)/2 x share to its TP (perStack from the depth-1 on-curve max HP).
  The term is exactly 0 for every other archetype (asserted by BalanceAuditTest
  deathSplashIsPricedOnlyWhereItExistsAndTheWretchStaysInBand); R-ENEMY bands the
  sum. Sump Wretch: 9.6 + 4.8 = 14.4 (CHAFF).
  OVERRIDE RECORD: GOLDEN_RATIO_* bands and the hand-set ENEMY_TP_* bands (chaff
  16-34 | soldier 36-66 | bruiser 70-120 | mini-elite 160-310) -> the rules above.
  Every archetype's depth-1 HP/damage was rebased (BalanceConfig SECTION 2 note);
  damage sits at the LOW end of each band because the heal economy absorbs it.
  RE-STATED by balance-overhaul order 3 (CP2) — MATCHUPS. Two eHP readings now exist:
    NEUTRAL eHP (EnemyType.neutralEffectiveHitPoints) — the matchup-free survivability
      primitive. Part 2 (the R8 hit bands), R-LADDER and R-CARD-BREAKPOINT read it: the
      box describes the fight with an answer that is NOT resisted, and R-LADDER's
      reference stays the Assault Rifle vs a neutral target.
    PRICED eHP (EnemyType.effectiveHitPoints) — neutral eHP / referenceMultiplier, with
      referenceMultiplier = 1 + w x (MatchupTable.multiplier(BALLISTIC, trait) - 1) and
      w = BalanceConfig.MATCHUP_TP_REFERENCE_WEIGHT (GameMath.matchupReferenceMultiplier /
      traitAdjustedEnemyEffectiveHitPoints). Part 1 (the TP bands), XP and the encounter
      budget read it: an armoured archetype costs what it takes the reference
      generalist to kill, blended toward neutral because the on-curve player usually
      carries a second gun. Every archetype stays inside its role TP band for any
      w in [0, 0.83] (the Verdant Spiresower, PLATED, is the binding row).
      w SHIPS AT 0 — a DECISION (balance-overhaul order 3, CP7), so priced eHP ==
      neutral eHP. Re-fit with TACTICAL switching by matchup and the CP7 planned-heal
      re-fit: w = 0.25 -> S-SUPPLY 0.83, w = 0.5 -> 0.81, both over the 0.80 cap (w = 0
      -> 0.78). Pricing the rifle's armour penalty makes the planner trade armoured
      bodies for unresisted ones, which the sim's two-gun kit beats more easily. Revisit
      in the order-7 feel pass, with S-SUPPLY, once played kits carry more classes.
  OVERRIDE RECORD: "enemy eHP == raw HP / no mitigation" in TP pricing -> the priced
  eHP above; the R8 hit bands' eHP -> explicitly the NEUTRAL eHP (unchanged numbers).

R-CARD (RuleKind.CARD_BUDGET) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 5 (CP2)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: "every level-up card prices into LEVEL_UP_BUDGET_PP (12) +/- 15%"
  and the TOTAL-PP INVARIANT ("total power at level L = L x 12 PP for every build")
  -> REPLACED by R-PERK PK1 FELT / PK2 FOCUSED (a perk rank must be felt on its tag's
  niche, and stay focused there) plus R-CARRY (a bounded spread between builds).
  UpgradeCard / UpgradeCardDeck / UpgradeCardPool, RuleKind.CARD_BUDGET and the
  card constants are deleted; level-ups draw tagged perks from progression/PerkDeck.
  The expected player's card term is now EXPECTED_PERK_POWER_PP_PER_PICK (12, SECTION
  24) x (characterLevel - 1) — the same number, so R-LADDER and every boss / route
  rule reading the expected player are unchanged. BalanceAuditTest's card-budget test
  asserts BalanceSchema.perkFeltResults(). (Full R-PERK text: the R-PERK entry below.)

R-HEAL (RuleKind.HEAL_PRICING) — RE-STATED by balance-overhaul order 1 (R10)
  Every heal/armour pickup restores a FRACTION of the max it refills, resolved at
  the moment of use (GameMath.fractionOfMaximum): 'H' medkit 0.45 max HP, '+' stim
  0.18, 'a' shard 0.15 max armour, 'A' vest 0.60. The rule bands that fraction:
    small pickups ('+', 'a'):  [0.10, 0.25] of max
    large pickups ('H', 'A'):  [0.40, 0.70] of max
  Survival turns bought on the model floor stay in the rule detail as information.
  WHY: a heal must keep its meaning at every depth while max HP grows with
  character level (R9) — a flat 90 HP was a full reset at level 1 and a sip at 20.
  OVERRIDE RECORD: flat MEDKIT_STIM_HEAL 32 / MEDKIT_FULL_HEAL 90 /
  ARMOUR_SHARD_VALUE 14 / ARMOUR_VEST_VALUE 62 and the survival-turns bands
  (small [2,6], large [8,16]) -> the fractions and fraction bands above.

R-TELEGRAPH (RuleKind.TELEGRAPH)
  Registered attack list: BalanceSchema.telegraphAttacks(). No UN-readable
  (readable=NONE) attack may exceed TELEGRAPH_MAX_UNTELEGRAPHED_HIT_FRACTION
  (25% of reference eHP ~= 51 dmg) in one hit. The 35% hard cap
  (BOSS_HARD_SINGLE_HIT_FRACTION) now applies to boss verbs via R-BOSS-VERB-CAP
  (order 6) — boss verbs are DERIVED per depth, not flat, so they are checked there
  (at their derived values) rather than in this flat, depth-scaled trash-mob list.
  WHY: a death must feel like "I made a mistake", never a dice roll. Readable
  kinds: TELE (wind-up), LANE (cardinal-line tell), FACE (positional counter).
  NOTE: any NEW attack path must be added to the registered list — the audit
  only checks attacks it knows about.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (balance-overhaul order 4, CP1): the "Shell Brute CHARGE" and
  "Plague Hulk blast" rows are REMOVED from the list — heavies are R-HEAVY's domain
  (below), sized as a fraction of on-curve max HP, and those rows read constants that
  no longer exist. Every other row and the 25% un-telegraphed cap are unchanged.

R-HEAVY (RuleKind.HEAVY) — balance-overhaul order 4 (NEW; extends R-TELEGRAPH)
  Registered data: enemy/HeavyAttack (CHARGE, LANCE, AREA_STRIKE, SELF_DESTRUCT) — the
  HEAVY flag is per-constant data (damageFraction, minimumCommitTurns, displayName);
  EnemyType.heavyWindUp() and SpecialAbility.heavyAttack() say which archetype / verb
  resolves which heavy. BalanceSchema.heavyResults(), every HeavyAttack x depth 1..25:
    (a) DAMAGE   heavyDamageAtDepth(fraction, expectedPlayerAtDepth(d).maxHealth,
                 HEAVY_DAMAGE_SCALE) / on-curve max HP in [HEAVY_DAMAGE_FRACTION_MIN 0.40,
                 HEAVY_DAMAGE_FRACTION_MAX 0.60]. Defaults: CHARGE/LANCE 0.50, AREA_STRIKE
                 0.45, SELF_DESTRUCT 0.55.
    (b) COMMIT   minimumCommitTurns() >= 1 (what the code guarantees: 1 for charge/lance/
                 slam, the brace countdown for the self-destruct).
    (c) GUARD    fraction x GUARD_FRONT_HEAVY (0.20) <= HEAVY_GUARDED_MAX_FRACTION (0.12).
                 (Wired by CP3: Player.applyDirectionalDamage(.., heavy) applies GUARD_FRONT_HEAVY.)
  GUARD + ARMOUR (CP3 — OVERRIDE RECORD): GUARD_FRONT_MULTIPLIER 0.35 is DELETED (BalanceConfig +
  GameBalance shim) -> GUARD_FRONT_ORDINARY 0.50 (ordinary front-arc hits) / GUARD_FRONT_HEAVY 0.20
  (heavy). A heavy front-guarded hit RIPOSTES: attacker stunned next turn + VULNERABLE
  VULNERABLE_DURATION turns (none vs bosses / the dying self-destruct). ARMOUR_ABSORB_FRACTION 0.50 ->
  0.67; a heavy costs armour absorbed x ARMOUR_HEAVY_SHRED_MULTIPLIER 1.5 (GameMath.armorShredCost);
  STATUS SHIELD: armour > 0 blocks enemy WEAK/SLOWED/BLINDED/POISONED at ARMOUR_STATUS_COST_FRACTION
  0.10 x max armour (GameMath.armorStatusShieldCost); hazard tiles unblocked.
  Live damage: Enemy.heavyDamage(kind) = the fraction of the on-curve max HP at the
  enemy's spawn depth (stored at spawn) x EMPOWERED x WEAK, min 1 — the committed
  predicted number and the executed hit are the same value. HEAVY_DAMAGE_SCALE (1.0) is
  the ordinary order-7 feel knob. TP pricing: GameMath.heavyExpectedDamageForPricing(
  heavy damage at the depth-1 player, HEAVY_EXPECTED_AVOIDANCE 0.6) = 40% of the hit.
  WHY: nothing in combat was big enough to demand a response; a heavy must be avoided,
  not tanked (owner). Avoidability geometry (danger tiles) is CP2.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (CP1): SHELL_BRUTE_CHARGE_DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER 2.4, SHELL_BRUTE_CHARGE_STOP_SHORT_CHANCE
  0.75 (a reached heavy charge now ALWAYS lands — staying on the lane costs the fraction),
  PLAGUE_HULK_SELF_DESTRUCT_BLAST_DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER 3.0 + _MAX 68, AREA_STRIKE_DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER
  1.35 and GameMath.specialEquivalentAreaStrike are DELETED -> the heavy fractions above.

R-DEPTH (RuleKind.DEPTH_COUPLING) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 1
  OVERRIDE RECORD: the depth-coupling ratio (playerPowerAtDepthV2 / enemy threat
  scale in [0.9, 1.2] at 1..15) is REPLACED by R-LADDER L1 (on-curve TTK and TTD
  within +/-15% of depth 1 at every depth 1..25), which proves the stronger, per-
  fight property directly. GameMath.playerPowerAtDepth / playerPowerAtDepthV2 /
  depthCouplingRatio / gearRampAtDepth / expectedAbilityPowerPointsAtDepth /
  playerPowerAtLevelAndDepth and DEPTH_COUPLING_RATIO_* are deleted.

R-HAZARD (RuleKind.HAZARD) — balance-overhaul order 4 (NEW; CP6, H13 / H14)
  Hazards stay meaningful at every depth because their damage is a FRACTION, and barrels sit where the
  groups are so the player reads the room before charging in.
  Numbers (BalanceConfig SECTION 23 unless noted):
    HAZARD_BARREL_CENTRE_FRACTION 1.2 / HAZARD_BARREL_RING_FRACTION 0.6 — of the floor's REFERENCE SOLDIER
      max HP at that depth: GameMath.enemyHealthAtDepth(HAZARD_BARREL_REFERENCE_SOLDIER_BASE_HEALTH, depth),
      base = VOID_SHROUD_MAX_HEALTH 50 (the R-LADDER SOLDIER archetype; a rule line asserts they agree).
      The float HP is NOT rounded before the fraction, so the ring lands exactly on a Ghoul's own HP.
    HAZARD_BARREL_PLAYER_CENTRE_FRACTION 0.30 / HAZARD_BARREL_PLAYER_RING_FRACTION 0.15 — of the PLAYER's
      max HP (the existing barrel -> player path, only the number changed).
    GameMath.barrelBlastDamage(fraction, referenceMaxHealth) = max(1, round(fraction x reference)).
    HAZARD_FIRE_ENEMY_DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER (SECTION 12) 1.5 -> 2.5 (x FIRE matchup); HAZARD_TOXIC_ENEMY_STACKS_PER_TURN 2
      — toxic tiles AND the Plague Hulk cloud poison enemies at the enemy's own max HP.
    Placement: HAZARD_GROUP_ROOM_SHARE 0.40, HAZARD_GROUP_MIN_SIZE 3, HAZARD_GROUP_BARRELS_MIN 1 / _MAX 2,
      HAZARD_GROUP_BARREL_RADIUS_TILES 2 (see procedural-level-generation.txt).
  Rules asserted (BalanceSchema.hazardResults + hazardPlacementResults; BalanceAuditTest
  barrelsStayMeaningfulAtEveryDepthAndSitBesideGroups):
    - depths 1..25: a RING blast kills every CHAFF archetype (ring >= its scaled max HP) and never one-shots the
      reference soldier; the player's centre blast stays <= HEAVY_DAMAGE_FRACTION_MIN (0.40) of on-curve max HP;
    - the sweep: on every COMBAT / ELITE floor >= ceil(0.40 x hostable group rooms) hold barrels, and no other
      node type ever gets one.
  A6 ARITHMETIC (BalanceReport "BARREL BLAST", neutral matchup): at depth 10 the soldier has 161 HP, ring 97,
  centre 194. One ring hit leaves the soldier at 64 HP = 40%; two overlapping rings (two barrels) kill it
  (0%); every chaff dies to one ring (Ghoul 97 vs 97 is the tight one). The fractions are depth-flat, so the
  same 40% / 0% holds at depths 1, 5, 15 and 25. "Leaves a soldier at ~a fifth" is NOT met by the spec's 0.6
  ring (it would need ~0.8) — reported to the owner, not retuned.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (order 4, CP6): BalanceConfig.EXPLOSION_DAMAGE (flat 12) and EffectConstants.EXPLOSION_DAMAGE
  DELETED; HAZARD_THREAT_TURNS_STOOD (2) DELETED — hazard TP (GameMath.hazardTileThreatPoints) now prices a
  fraction of the on-curve depth-1 max HP over the burn window HAZARD_FIRE_BURN_TURNS (2, same numbers as
  before); HAZARD_FIRE_ENEMY_DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER 1.5 -> 2.5; enemies on toxic tiles 1 stack/turn -> 2 (the CP4
  TEMPORARY). LevelGeneratorSnapshotTest re-baselined (barrels now stamped on generated COMBAT / ELITE floors).

R-XP-PACE (RuleKind.XP_PACE) — XP pacing is a RULE, not a hope (order 4; horizon 1..25 since balance-overhaul order 1)
  RE-FIT (balance-overhaul order 1): horizon 1..RUN_FINAL_DEPTH (25), and the
  geometric XP growth follows the NEW threat compound: 1.118 -> 1.2495
  (= ENEMY_HEALTH_GROWTH 1.139 x ENEMY_DAMAGE_GROWTH 1.097), so the yield stays
  depth-stable at ~1.11. For every depth: floorRosterXp(d) / xpRequired(expectedLevel(d)) in
  [XP_FLOOR_YIELD_MIN, MAX] = [1.0, 1.3] level-ups per floor. Per-enemy XP is
  DERIVED — xpReward = XP_PER_THREAT_POINT * enemyThreatAtDepth (one knob replaces
  13 hand-set constants) — and the XP curve is GEOMETRIC (base * growth^(level-1),
  growth 1.118 tied to the enemy threat compound), so roster XP and the level
  requirement grow at the SAME rate and the yield is depth-STABLE (~1.11). CATCH-UP
  (GameMath.catchUpScaledXp): a player > 1 level below expected gets XP * 1.5,
  forward-only. WHY: leveling must be FELT and PACED — a floor reliably buys ~1
  level at every depth, and a fallen-behind run can recover.

R-CARD-BREAKPOINT (RuleKind.CARD_BREAKPOINT) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 5 (CP2)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: folded into R-PERK PK1 (every perk's max rank crosses >= 1 whole
  TTK / TTD step on its niche, or its D4 resource step). RuleKind.CARD_BREAKPOINT and
  the card breakpoint helpers are deleted; BalanceAuditTest's card-breakpoint test
  asserts BalanceSchema.perkBreakpointResults(). The retired rule, for the record:
  Every level-up card must cross >= 1 INTEGER combat breakpoint vs the region
  soldier in some region: an OFFENCE card shaves a whole turn off the kill
  (GameMath.turnsToKillBreakpointGain), a DEFENCE card survives a whole extra hit
  (turnsToDie gain). Cards keep their equal PP budget (R-CARD); this rule proves
  the budget is spent where it FEELS different next fight, not just on the sheet.
  RE-FIT (balance-overhaul order 1): measured against the ON-CURVE ExpectedPlayer
  at every depth 1..25 (was the gear-curve DPT and the fixed 205 eHP, 1..15);
  offence cards apply as a %-lift of the on-curve DPT (the model's convention),
  defence cards as flat eHP. The reference soldier (Void Shroud) is unchanged.

R-PERK (RuleKind.PERK) — balance-overhaul order 5 (REPLACES R-CARD, R-CARD-BREAKPOINT and the
  TOTAL-PP INVARIANT). OVERRIDE RECORD (2026-10-11): R-CARD ("every level-up card prices into
  LEVEL_UP_BUDGET_PP 12 +/- 15%"), the invariant ("total power at level L = L x 12 PP for every build")
  and R-CARD-BREAKPOINT ("every card crosses >= 1 integer breakpoint vs the region soldier") are
  REPLACED by the rule below plus R-CARRY. A perk is no longer priced to be EQUAL; it is priced to be
  FELT inside its style and to stay there. Evaluated by util/BuildValuationModel on the order-3
  reference scenarios at BUILD_PERK_REFERENCE_DEPTHS {5, 15}; every perk's NICHE is its tag's metric
  (BuildNicheMetric: SCENARIO_TURNS on the tag's reference scenario + weapon, SCENARIO_AMMO,
  BARREL_OPENER_TURNS, DUEL_SURVIVALS / FLOOR_SURVIVALS for BULWARK and VITALITY, SUPPLY_* / HEAL_SUPPLY
  for SCAVENGER and Field Medic; a "*" in the PERKS table marks a per-perk niche override).
    PK1 FELT        every rank improves its niche, vs the rank below, by >= PERK_FELT_MIN_GAIN (10%) at
                    every reference depth; and by its max rank the perk crosses >= 1 WHOLE TTK or TTD step
                    on the niche at some reference depth (WILDFIRE, DECISION D4: an integer RESOURCE step —
                    fuel / sprays spent to clear S5 — because its fights end inside one turn).
    PK2 FOCUSED     no non-VITALITY perk at max rank improves ALL eight reference scenarios by more than
                    PERK_FOCUSED_MAX_GAIN (10%); a VITALITY perk improves no scenario by more than
                    PERK_VITALITY_MAX_GAIN_PER_RANK (10%) per rank (VITALITY is the generic tag, so it is
                    capped instead of focused).
  WAIVERS (SECTION 4): DECISION D6 — WILDFIRE's PK1 r2 / r3 FELT rows at d5 and d15 and its D4
  breakpoint row; every reference group fight ends inside the first spray or two, so burn DURATION (what
  ranks 2-3 buy, D5) is invisible to the scenario math. Rank-1 FELT (+200% on S5) and PK2 stay enforced.
  Expires: a long-group reference scenario exists (order 7 feel pass).
  WHY: equal-value generic cards produced the same build every run (owner, J3). Felt-in-niche,
  focused-off-niche perks make "lean in or pivot?" a real question. Asserted by BalanceAuditTest
  (everyPerkRankIsFeltOnItsNicheReplacingTheCardBudget, everyPerkCrossesABreakpointByItsMaxRank, + PK2).

R-KEYSTONE (RuleKind.KEYSTONE) — balance-overhaul order 5
  Each of the nine keystones (offered at KEYSTONE_CHARACTER_LEVELS 5 / 10 / 15 / 20 instead of that
  level's perk offer) improves its niche by >= KEYSTONE_NICHE_MIN_GAIN (30%) at every reference depth AND
  worsens at least one OTHER probe (a scenario, a weapon outside the tag, the duel, the floor, a heal) by
  >= KEYSTONE_COST_MIN_LOSS (10%) at some depth. WHY: a keystone is a rule-changer with a real cost —
  without the cost it is just a bigger perk, and the run is not "committed to a shape". Live values: the
  KEYSTONES table in SECTION 6 (niche 30-101%, cost 10.6-62.7%).

R-CARRY (RuleKind.BUILD_CARRY) — balance-overhaul order 5 (REPLACES the total-PP invariant)
  For every BuildTag, the expected player at BUILD_CARRY_DEPTH (15) holding that tag's perks at the
  expected rank count (round(BUILD_EXPECTED_FOCUS_SHARE 0.6 x perk picks), capped at the tag's rank
  capacity; perk picks = levels - 1 - keystone levels = 11 at d15) plus that tag's keystone has a carry
  score = kill-speed lift x survival lift (geometric means over S1-S8, a two-gun kit) within
  1 + BUILD_CARRY_TOLERANCE (best / worst <= 1.20) of every other tag's. WHY: no trap build and no
  dominant build — a bounded spread is what makes a choice real while keeping all nine viable.
  WAIVER (DECISION D3): SCAVENGER is an ECONOMY tag (supply, shop prices, carrier drops — no turn to kill
  or hit to die), so the combat carry under-reads it (1.09 vs the combat tags' ~1.75-2.08). Its row is
  printed, flagged WAIVED, and excluded from best / worst. Expires when order 6 re-derives the economy with
  SCAVENGER in the expected player. Live: eight-tag spread 1.189 (limit 1.20) — VITALITY (2.081, pinned
  near its PK2 floor) is the best, MARKSMAN (1.751) the worst; ~1% headroom (see the knowledge doc).

R-RELIC (RuleKind.RELIC) — relics-run-changing-artifacts (CP5)
  Every non-CURSED relic (RelicCatalog, 36 rows; docs/relic-system.txt) has a GAIN — its R-CARRY-style carry lift
  (BuildValuationModel.carryOf over BuildStats.ofEffects(its effects) on its affinity tag's two-gun kit, else the
  best pair), or the SUPPLY_TOTAL ratio for an economy (valuation SUPPLY) relic — inside its rarity x valuation band
  at EVERY RELIC_REFERENCE_DEPTHS depth (5 / 15 / 25). CARRY: COMMON [1.03, 1.08], RARE [1.06, 1.13], LEGENDARY
  [1.10, 1.22]; SUPPLY: COMMON [1.06, 1.20], RARE [1.15, 1.35] (BalanceConfig SECTION 26). WHY the floor: it is the
  "felt" test — a relic whose effect kinds the valuation does not read prices at exactly 1.00 and fails, so an
  unpriced kind cannot ship. WHY SUPPLY: the carry conversion under-reads supply (the reason SCAVENGER's R-CARRY row is
  waived). LEGENDARY top widened 1.20 -> 1.22 by the CP5 hub licence (a band new in this feature; SECOND HEART's
  Second Wind flag alone prices 1.205 at d5). Asserted by BalanceAuditTest.everyRelicPricesIntoItsRarityBand.

R-RELIC-CURSE (RuleKind.RELIC_CURSE) — relics-run-changing-artifacts (CP5)
  Every CURSED relic: (a) its gain is inside the CURSED band for its valuation at every reference depth (CARRY
  [1.05, 1.18], SUPPLY [1.20, 1.60]); AND (b) its COST effects alone (PerkModifier.isCostAt, priced by
  RelicValuationModel.costLoss -> BuildValuationModel.worstLoss with a NULL niche, i.e. every probe counts) cost >=
  RELIC_CURSED_MIN_LOSS (0.10) on some probe at some reference depth — the R-KEYSTONE cost idiom. MEASURE: the cost
  effects ALONE, because worstLoss over the whole relic reported spurious losses for pure gains (an Incinerator-S2
  cell) and would have let a free "curse" pass. WHY: a bargain with no price is just a bigger relic; and a
  cursed card is only ever met behind a seal the player chose to break. Asserted by
  BalanceAuditTest.everyCursedRelicTradesAGainForARealCost. Live: cost losses 0.107 .. 0.626.

R-RELIC-STACK (RuleKind.RELIC_STACK) — relics-run-changing-artifacts (CP5)
  At every BossTable depth (5/10/15/20/25): expectedHandLift(d) <= the WORST non-waived R-CARRY tag score ("a run's
  expected relics never out-carry one committed build"), AND bestHandLift(d) <= the BEST non-waived R-CARRY score x
  (1 + BUILD_CARRY_TOLERANCE) ("even the luckiest hand stays inside the build spread"). Both ceilings are read LIVE
  from the R-CARRY results — no new number. bestHandLift is AFFINITY-CONSISTENT: the non-affine relics plus the
  affine relics of at most ONE BuildTag (a two-gun build cannot use every tag-affine relic), best over every tag,
  LEGENDARY at most one per boss passed, CURSED only through node offers. expectedRelicCount(d) = 1 (the start relic)
  + bosses passed + (d - 1) x nodeOfferRatePerFloor() (0.00791 per floor with RELIC_CACHE at weight 3). WHY: relics are
  BONUS power accounted beside the unchanged on-curve ExpectedPlayer (decision D3); this is the cap that keeps them from
  becoming a second progression curve. Live: expected hand 1.086 / 1.191 / 1.320 / 1.479 / 1.695 at d5..d25 against the
  1.751 ceiling (THIN at d25: 3.2%); best hand 1.175 .. 2.290 against 2.497. Asserted by
  BalanceAuditTest.noRelicHandOutCarriesACommittedBuild.

R-RELIC-SOURCE (RuleKind.RELIC_SOURCE) — relics-run-changing-artifacts (CP5)
  Structural: each rarity holds >= RELIC_OFFER_MAX_CARDS (3) non-affine relics (an offer always fills); every
  RelicSource weight row sums to 1 (+/- 1e-4) with one weight per allowed rarity; no duplicate relic ids; every CURSED
  relic has a cost line AND at least one cost effect; RELIC_PERK_RANK_EQUIVALENT_LIFT (1.08) is within
  RELIC_PERK_RANK_EQUIVALENT_TOLERANCE (0.03) of the worst committed build's per-rank lift ((worst non-waived carry)^
  (1 / (ranks + keystone)); live 1.0725, MARKSMAN) — the price anchor offer pricing and the RELICS bands stand on;
  nodeOfferRatePerFloor() > 0. R-RELIC-SOURCE carries one "resolves" row per relic (rarity, trigger, valuation, banded — the line COVERAGE carried until balance-overhaul order 8). Existing rules
  that now read relics: R-ROUTE-PRICED (the RELIC_CACHE NodeEconomics row; the priced offer is progress PP =
  GameMath.relicPowerPoints of the source pool's mean lift), R-MYSTERY-EV v2 (RELIC_CACHE at weight 3: 1.2996 of the
  1.30 ceiling — weight 4 breaks it at 1.3022; the idea's floor of 4 was unreachable), R-HONEST-SAFE, R-EVENT-VALUE
  (quarantine_locker: SELL THE SEAL alone meets it). Asserted by BalanceAuditTest.theRelicPoolAlwaysFillsAnOffer.
  Tuning order on a failure (D7): that relic's own SECTION 26 magnitude; then MYSTERY_WEIGHT_RELIC_CACHE; NEVER an
  existing pre-relic number or band.

R-SCARCITY (RuleKind.SCARCITY) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 2 (CP3c)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: the model-floor band (floor-wide S in [0.75, 0.95], per-weapon S
  < 0.60, net drain 5-15%) priced DICE — per-room ammo chances, per-kill drop chances
  — that no longer exist. REPLACED by R-SUPPLY (below): every generated floor's supply
  is PLANNED from its own roster and tracked against that plan. The SECTION 10 model
  floor survives only as a printed example (BalanceReport "SUPPLY PLAN — model floor
  example"). The scarcity ROW registry stays — it is the AmmoType coverage check and
  the per-unit damage table the planner prices against.

R-SUPPLY (RuleKind.SUPPLY) — supply tracks demand (balance-overhaul order 2)
  Every generator builds through ONE pipeline (layout -> SupplySlotSurvey ->
  encounter -> SupplyPlanner.plan -> SupplyPlanner.place). The plan is derived from
  the roster the floor ACTUALLY fields and the node's NodeSupplySpec (S2-S10); it
  never reads the player's current HP or ammo. Asserted twice:
    PLANNER LEVEL (BalanceSchema.supplyPlannerResults) — every spec x depths
      {1,5,10,15,20,25} x 30 seeds, planned against a real roster and placed on a
      synthetic eight-room floor.
    GENERATOR SWEEP (BalanceSchema.supplySweepResults, A2) — every generator x every
      node type it serves x the same depths x 30 seeds, read back from the
      FloorContentReport the shared FloorPopulator attaches to each Level.
  Per cell (worst case over its seeds):
    heal floor   >= SUPPLY_HEAL_FLOOR_FRACTION (0.45 max HP = one 'H') of HEAL value
                 placed reachable WITHOUT a keycard (not BOSS / REST / REGION_GATE)
    first half   >= 50% of that heal floor within half the farthest keycard-free walk
    tracking     |placed - plan| <= max(10% of plan, half the category's largest
                 pickup) for AMMO / HEAL / ARMOUR / CREDITS ("only rounding to whole
                 pickups may move it"); nothing unplaced
    spread       no room / chamber holds more than max(1, floor(35% x count)) of a
                 category's ground pickups (count basis; corridors are not rooms)
    carriers     ~25% (+/-0.08) of the eligible ammo / non-floor heal pickups ride
                 on enemies (S6)
  RESERVE BANKS (BalanceSchema.ammoBankingResults, balance-overhaul order 3 A-1): every
    ammo type's full reserve banks AmmoType.getBankingFloorsTarget() floors of the model
    floor's demand (cap x damage per unit / 502), +/- AMMO_BANKING_FLOORS_TOLERANCE 0.15:
    BULLETS 1.0, SHELLS 1.5, CELLS 1.5, ROCKETS 1.5, SLUGS 1.0.
  A-1 AMMO GENEROSITY (balance-overhaul order 3, CP4): the S2 split is WEIGHTED per
    AmmoType (getSupplyGenerosity — BULLETS 0.8, SHELLS 1.3, CELLS 1.0, ROCKETS 1.0,
    SLUGS 0.9) and RE-NORMALISED (GameMath.normalisedAmmoShare), so a floor's total
    planned ammo damage is unchanged and only the mix moves. With only the rifle carried
    the shares go 70 / 7.5 x4 -> bullets 64%, shells 11.1%, cells 8.6%, rockets 8.6%,
    slugs 7.7%. MAPPING: the spec's GRENADES are AmmoType.ROCKETS; the spec's FUEL has no
    type of its own — the Incinerator burns CELLS (shared with the Plasma Rifle and the
    Arc Cannon), which keep 1.0.
    OVERRIDE RECORD: the order-2 unweighted 70/30 carried / off-type split -> weighted
    and normalised as above. RESERVE CAPS re-fitted to the per-type banking targets:
    BULLETS 38 -> 25 (1.51 -> 1.00 floors), SHELLS 11 (1.45, unchanged since CP3b's
    17 -> 11), CELLS 27 (1.51, unchanged), ROCKETS 18 -> 21 (1.29 -> 1.51), SLUGS 6 -> 7
    (0.90 -> 1.05). RESERVE_BANKING_FLOORS_TARGET (1.5, the aggregate R-BOSS-AMMO reads)
    is unchanged.
  WHY: three generators placed loot with three different codes built from
  independent per-room chances — a rooms floor rolled ~8 medkits and ~10 ammo boxes
  at depth 1, a cave ~0.45 medkits (only inside a MEDICAL_BAY chamber that might not
  exist). "Sometimes too many resources, other times too little; I die in the caves
  because they have no medkits." Supply is now what the fights cost, minus a bite.

R-DOT (RuleKind.DOT_UNIQUENESS) — RESTATED by balance-overhaul order 4 (CP4)
  Exactly ONE definition per DoT, in TWO families, each defined once and re-exported
  byte-for-byte:
    STATUS DoTs (hazard fire, acid / toxic poison) are FRACTIONS of the host's max HP,
    resolved at application time via GameMath.statusTickDamage(fraction, maxHealth, stacks)
    = max(1, round(fraction x maxHealth)) x stacks: BalanceConfig.BURN_TICK_FRACTION 0.03,
    BalanceConfig.POISON_TICK_FRACTION 0.015 (SECTION 23).
    WEAPON burn (Incendiary, Incinerator) is R-ROLE-fitted damage and keeps ONE base:
    BalanceConfig.WEAPON_BURN_BASE_PER_TURN (4f, the same value as the old unified base);
    INCENDIARY_BURN_PER_TURN_BASE must BE it (it references it).
  The pre-order-4 flat BURN_DAMAGE_PER_TURN (4) and POISON_DAMAGE_PER_STACK (2) are DELETED
  and must not reappear in BalanceConfig or EffectConstants (the rule checks by reflection).
  Structurally, every field in a shim class (GameBalance, EffectConstants, EnemyConstants,
  WeaponConstants, ItemConstants, LevelGenConstants, Constants) that shares a name with a
  BalanceConfig field must re-export it byte-for-byte — reflection catches any re-introduced
  divergent literal.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (order 4, CP4): SECTION 8 status magnitudes replaced — BURN 4/turn flat ->
  BURN_TICK_FRACTION 0.03 of host max HP; POISON 2/stack flat -> POISON_TICK_FRACTION 0.015 of
  host max HP per stack; POISON_MAX_STACKS 5 -> 6 (stacks decay by 1 a turn; POISON_DURATION and
  HAZARD_TOXIC_POISON_TURNS deleted — poison's turns left = its stacks); WEAK_DAMAGE_PERCENT
  25 -> 35 with EnemyConstants.DEBUFF_WEAK_DURATION_TURNS 2 -> 3 (ONE WEAK percent for both
  directions); new POISON_STACKS_PER_ACID_HIT 2, POISON_STACKS_PER_TOXIC_TURN 2 (player),
  LANCE_SLOW_DURATION_TURNS 2 (the Rimeshell lance SLOWS on a connecting hit). Status pricing
  (specialEquivalentDebuffWeak) reads the new WEAK numbers; the hazard read-out in BalanceReport
  prices fire/toxin at the PLAYER side at the on-curve depth-1 max HP. Acceptance A5: poison per
  stack per turn is a constant fraction of max HP at any depth (130 HP -> 2 = 1.54%; 434 HP at
  depth 15 -> 7 = 1.61%; nominal 1.5%, differing only by rounding).
  HISTORY: two divergent BURN definitions coexisted (ability base 3 vs applied
  4); unification kept the EffectConstants value (4) because it was the one the
  game actually applied. Order 4 then split status burn (fraction of HP) from weapon burn (4).

R-FLAGS (RuleKind.FLAGS)
  No live test/debug flags: every static boolean whose name contains TEST or
  DEBUG (scanned by reflection across all constant files) must be false, plus
  the explicitly-named GameBalance.START_ROOM_ANY_TIER_ENABLED.
  WHY: START_ROOM_ANY_TIER_ENABLED=true shipped legendaries into the start room
  for weeks; RouteMapConstants.BOSS_TEST_ALWAYS_CLICKABLE=true let testers skip
  regions. Both were set false by order 1; this rule makes the leak class
  structurally impossible to repeat.

ADMISSION (RuleKind.ADMISSION) — the content balance contract (balance-overhaul order 8)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: REPLACES COVERAGE (RuleKind.COVERAGE, new-game-balancr order 1 — "every weapon item,
  consumable, ammo type and enemy role is classified"). Its four checks are FOLDED, strictly stronger, into
  C-WEAPON, C-ITEM, C-AMMO and C-ENEMY / C-VERB; its later relic line ("rarity x valuation resolves a band")
  moved to R-RELIC-SOURCE as "R-RELIC-SOURCE resolves <id>". RuleKind.COVERAGE no longer exists.
  Eighteen C-rules, one per content kind K1-K18 (BalanceSchema.AdmissionRule; each constant carries its
  evaluator, so adding a rule is one constant). Each iterates the game's own collection and returns ONE
  result per content item, subject "C-<KIND>: <id>"; a failure prints "ADMISSION C-<KIND>: <id> — <every
  missing item>". A C-rule checks DECLARED + PRICED + REACHED-BY-ITS-BAND-RULE, never the band verdict,
  which is why it can never be waived (G5: waive() throws for ADMISSION). What each rule checks, the
  checklists and the worked examples: SECTION 5. BalanceAuditTest runs one test per C-rule.
  WHY: the necrotic faction shipped un-priced and silently corrupted every floor budget for weeks. COVERAGE
  caught four content kinds; the series added a dozen more models (ladder, supply, roles, heavies, builds,
  bosses, route, shop) that one careless addition could break just as quietly.

R-GEARGATE (RuleKind.GEAR_GATE) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 1
  OVERRIDE RECORD: the per-region gear step (GEAR_CURVE_PER_REGION 1.35^region) and
  its golden-ratio gate at the region-2 entry are REPLACED by R-LADDER L2/L4 — a
  per-FLOOR ladder (weapon level vs threat level) whose lag penalty is proven at
  every depth 1..25, a stronger form of "stagnation is punished". What SURVIVES: the
  supply half — dropped weapons still roll their tier from WEAPON_DROP_TIER_*_BY_REGION
  (now also the expected rarity of the on-curve player) and THE PITY RULE
  (GUARANTEED_UPGRADE_PER_REGION) stayed until balance-overhaul order 2 CP6 re-homed it as the
  two-floor WEAPON CADENCE (S9; see the CP6 change-log entry).
  GameMath.gearCurveAtDepth / expectedPlayerDamagePerTurn and GEAR_CURVE_PER_REGION
  are deleted (GEAR_CURVE_REGION_BAND_SIZE stays: every per-region table indexes by it).

R-ABILITY (RuleKind.ABILITY_BUDGET) — tier is a priced ability budget (order 2)
  Rarity NEVER raises a weapon's POWER band (that was a role property of the since-retired R-WEAPON) —
  it buys ABILITIES [OVERRIDDEN by balance-overhaul order 1: rarity now ALSO
  multiplies damage — R5 — while keeping this ability budget], and abilities are now PRICED in the SAME power points (PP =
  %-of-reference DPT/eHP) the level-up cards use (GameMath.abilityPowerPoints;
  an ability's magnitude converted to a %-of-reference, discounted by a
  utilisation weight for how often it applies). Each tier has an ability-PP
  budget: COMMON 0 | UNCOMMON 6 | RARE 12 | EPIC 20 | LEGENDARY 30 (±20%). The
  WeaponRoller fills abilities greedily and stops before the tier ceiling, so
  every roll — spawn OR shop tier-upgrade — fits its budget by construction. The
  rule asserts (a) every ability is priced to a finite, non-negative PP at every
  level and no ability's max-level PP exceeds the legendary ceiling, and (b) the
  tier budgets are monotonic with rarity; BalanceAuditTest additionally sweeps
  real rolls across seeds/levels/tiers and confirms no roll (in particular no
  legendary) exceeds its ceiling. WHY: the whole ability catalogue shipped as
  unpriced "PLACEHOLDER — flag for playtesting" magnitudes; this converts it to
  priced content in one pass. Magnitudes may still be tuned freely — the price
  recomputes from the magnitude, so tuning stays free.

  RE-STATED by balance-overhaul order 5 (CP4) — OVERRIDE RECORD (2026-10-11): the order-2 tier CEILINGS
  above (UNCOMMON 6 / RARE 12 / EPIC 20 / LEGENDARY 30, filled greedily) and WeaponTier's slot counts
  (0/1/2/3/5) are REPLACED by B5 / B6:
    COUNTS     every roll rolls its tier's ability count IN FULL: COMMON 0, UNCOMMON 1, RARE 2,
               EPIC 3 (exactly one MAJOR), LEGENDARY 3 (one MAJOR) + its SIGNATURE on top
               (TIER_ABILITY_COUNT_* / TIER_MAJOR_COUNT_*, SECTION 24).
    BUDGETS    UNCOMMON 12 / RARE 25 / EPIC 40 / LEGENDARY 60 PP, and the roll's priced total must lie in
               budget x (1 -/+ TIER_ABILITY_PP_TOLERANCE 0.20) — a FLOOR as well as a ceiling (a RARE
               that rolls 3 PP is now a violation).
    RANKS      WeaponAbility.Rank STANDARD / MAJOR / SIGNATURE. A rank's expected weapon level is the
               shallowest depth r x 5 + 1 whose region max tier rolls it: STANDARD L1, MAJOR L11,
               SIGNATURE L16. MAJOR = EXECUTIONER, CLEAVE, OPENING_SALVO, BURST_FIRE (fixed 2-round).
    FELT (B6)  every surviving ability prices >= ABILITY_MIN_FELT_PP (8) at its expected level. 13 were
               CUT (HEMORRHAGE_HARVEST, VAMPIRIC_CRIT, BULWARK_ROUNDS, ADRENAL_SURGE, SECOND_WIND,
               SALVAGE_STRIKE, SCHOLARS_EDGE, QUICK_HANDS, FIELD_MEDIC_ROUNDS, CREDIT_FANG, EXTENDED_MAG,
               MARKSMANS_PATIENCE, RESONANT_ROUNDS) and OVERPENETRATION MERGED into ARMOR_PIERCE; the
               survivors were re-tuned (standards ~10.5 PP, MAJORs 12 -> 17.4, signatures 18) and each
               carries a BuildTag that counts toward the B3 SYNERGY test (Weapon.addAbilityBuildTags).
    SWEEP (A4) ABILITY_ROLL_SWEEP_SEEDS (30) x every tier x ABILITY_ROLL_SWEEP_DEPTHS {3, 12, 22} x melee
               and ranged — every roll meets its count, MAJOR and signature shape and stays in band.
  Asserted by BalanceAuditTest everySurvivingAbilityIsFeltAndEveryTierShapeFitsItsBand and
  everyRollMeetsItsTierCountAndStaysInsideItsBand. Live: ABILITY PRICING + TIERS in SECTION 6.

R-DENSITY (RuleKind.DENSITY) — full floors (balance-overhaul order 2)
  Read from the SAME generator sweep as R-SUPPLY (every generator x node type x depths
  {1,5,10,15,20,25} x 30 seeds), per cell, worst case over its seeds:
    bodies (E1)        COMBAT 12-16 at depth 1 rising linearly to 22-28 at depth 25
                       (BODY_TARGET_*), x the node's body scale (ELITE 1.05, CACHE / SHOP
                       0.35); fewest and most both inside the band
    shape (E4)         COMBAT / ELITE: >= 75% of enemies in groups of >= 2, >= 2 groups
                       of >= 3, <= 3 lone enemies
    first contact (E5) COMBAT / ELITE: the nearest member of a group of >= 2 within 18
                       walk tiles of the start (12 on depth 1), never in the start room
    footprint (E6)     OWNER OVERRIDE 2026-10-01. Every floor of ROOMS_MST / LINEAR_CORRIDOR
                       / CAVERN (any node type) within +/-15% of its target = the layout's
                       ORIGINAL walkable size (BalanceConfig FOOTPRINT_ORIGINAL_WALKABLE_*:
                       rooms 1130, corridor 700, cave 2100, measured before order 2) cut by
                       FOOTPRINT_REDUCTION_BY_DEPTH = 50% / 40% / 30% / 25% on floors 1-4 and
                       20% from floor 5 on. Targets: rooms 565 / 678 / 791 / 848 / 904,
                       corridor 350 / 420 / 490 / 525 / 560, cave 1050 / 1260 / 1470 / 1575 /
                       1680. REPLACED: the per-region 350-550 / 450-650 / 500-750 ranges (which
                       cut caves by 65-79%), ELITE's lower half, and A5's strict 350-550.
    density (E7)       REPORTED, no longer enforced (same override): the body targets were not
                       raised with the larger floors, so the [2.2, 4.0] band cannot hold —
                       measured COMBAT density now ~1.8-2.7 on rooms, ~2.9-4.5 on corridors,
                       ~0.9-1.4 on caves. BalanceReport's SUPPLY / DENSITY table still prints it.
  HOW THE GENERATORS MEET E6: level/FootprintPlanner rebuilds the floor (up to
  LevelGenConstants.FOOTPRINT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 24, a derived seed per attempt) at a footprint
  SCALE — a centred layout window for rooms and caves, the spine length for corridors —
  corrected by target / (the MEAN walkable tiles per unit scale over the attempts so far): a
  layout's size at one scale varies widely by seed (a corridor at scale 0.6 measures ~250-600),
  so correcting from the last attempt alone chased that noise. A build that did not field its
  body target ranks behind every build that did. The first such build within 0.7 x 15% of the
  target is kept, else the best ranked. Measured: 0 of 4050 floors (3 layouts x 5 node types x
  depths 1-5/10/15/20/25 x 30 seeds) outside the window or short of bodies.
  DECISION (CP5): the ELITE body scale is 1.05, not 1.2. At 1.2 the deep ELITE body band
  (26-34) could not fit the then lower-half footprint (500-625) under the 5.0 density ceiling; the
  ELITE's extra threat is carried by its 1.6x Threat-Point cap (heavier groups), which E1
  already intended, so the body count stays near COMBAT's.
  HOW THE PLANNER MEETS IT: level/EncounterBudgetPlanner fills the body target with
  registered GROUP TEMPLATES (level/EncounterGroupTemplates, reshaped by mixed-encounter-
  groups: RABBLE 2-3 PAIRS of different chaff, SKIRMISH 1-2 different soldiers + a chaff pair,
  FIRETEAM 2 different soldiers + a chaff pair, ESCORT bruiser + 1-2 chaff pairs [COMBAT
  anchor], BATTERY 1-2 ranged soldiers + a melee chaff pair, WARBAND mini-elite + 2 different
  soldiers [ELITE anchor, depth >= 3], HUNTER a lone bruiser / flanker [<= 20% of groups],
  BROOD 3 of ONE chaff [rare, see VARIETY], and CALM-only PAIR one chaff pair / LOOKOUT one
  soldier alone [<= 1 a floor]); the Threat-Point budget
  (x the region dial x the node's threat scale) is the CAP, and each pick aims at the
  threat-per-body still to spend, so an ELITE's 1.6x cap buys heavier groups (measured
  ~1.6x COMBAT's spent TP at every depth), not a crowd. level/EncounterPlacer puts each
  group in ONE room (cluster radius LEVEL_GEN_PACK_CLUSTER_RADIUS): first contact in the
  nearest room off the start (or the corridor pocket within the limit), the anchor group
  in the deepest room, every other group in a room no group holds (a LARGE room takes two).
  OVERRIDE RECORD (CP4): "enemy COUNT is depth-invariant (a known non-goal)" -> body
  targets grow with depth (E1). REPLACED and DELETED: ENCOUNTER_ANCHOR_BUDGET_FRACTION_MIN/
  MAX + _CEILING_FRACTION (-> template anchors), ENCOUNTER_ELITE_ANCHOR_FLOOR_CHANCE/
  _MIN_DEPTH (-> WARBAND on ELITE nodes only, GROUP_WARBAND_MIN_DEPTH 3), ENCOUNTER_PER_ROOM_
  TP_FRACTION_CAP 0.25 + ROOM_OPEN/CHOKEPOINT_TP_MULTIPLIER (-> GROUP_TP_FRACTION_CAP 0.35
  per group, anchor exempt), CHAFF_PACK_MIN/MAX + consecutive-run packs + the remainder pass
  (-> templates; GROUP_CHAFF_SLOT_MIN 2 keeps "chaff never spawns alone", asserted by the
  re-stated pack-coherence test), ENCOUNTER_MAX_SINGLE_TYPE_FRACTION (-> GROUP_MAX_SINGLE_
  TYPE_FRACTION, same 0.40). ENCOUNTER_BUDGET_FILL_TARGET_FRACTION 0.95 survives (the
  planner's aim; R-XP-PACE reads it). The linear generator's entrance room is now always
  small (LEVEL_GEN_SPINE_ENTRANCE_MAX_DIM 7): a 19x11 entrance put every non-start tile
  beyond 12 walk tiles, making E5 on depth 1 geometrically impossible.
  VARIETY (mixed-encounter-groups, owner request 2026-10-21: "rooms full of one enemy type,
  e.g. 5 Vortex Eyes, almost every time"). Measured before: 36-51% of COMBAT groups were one
  archetype and 82-95% of floors had a group of 4+ of one type. The rules (level/SlotFill +
  level/EncounterBehaviour carry them as per-constant data):
    V1 no archetype more than GROUP_MAX_SAME_TYPE_PER_GROUP (2) times in a group — chaff come in
       PAIRS, soldiers and heavies as SINGLES — except a BROOD (exactly GROUP_BROOD_SIZE 3);
    V2 an archetype fills one slot of one group only;
    V3 a non-BROOD group of GROUP_MIXED_BEHAVIOUR_MIN_SIZE (4)+ spans two EncounterBehaviours
       (RANGED / RUSHER = melee moving every turn / PLODDER = slower melee): the group's last
       draw is restricted to a missing behaviour; if the variety caps (not the pool) block it the
       draw fails and the planner tries another shape; the top-up keeps a grown group mixed
       (the threshold was 3; balanceSim tuning step 2 moved it to 4 — see TUNING below);
    V4 BROOD is eligible on GROUP_BROOD_FLOOR_CHANCE (0.25) of floors (rolled once per floor that
       fields enemies), at most GROUP_BROOD_MAX_PER_FLOOR (1), and only where 3 fits V6; never the
       first contact while another group of 2+ exists; picked on ~5-12% of COMBAT floors;
    V5 the top-up adds a DIFFERENT archetype (a new pair, then a new single), never a lone chaff;
    V6 no archetype past max(2, ceil(GROUP_MAX_SINGLE_TYPE_BODY_FRACTION 0.30 x body target))
       bodies while an alternative exists, beside the 0.40 TP share;
    V7 no room holds more than GROUP_ROOM_MAX_SAME_TYPE (3) of one archetype: a LARGE room's
       second group must respect it, the full-floor fallback picks the room it crowds least, and
       a floor out of rooms stands the group in the corridors; overflow spills per member.
  CALM floors' per-group cap (~45 TP at depth 1) cannot buy a 4-chaff RABBLE, so PAIR and
  LOOKOUT (GROUP_LOOKOUT_MAX_PER_FLOOR 1, the odd body pairs cannot field) keep CACHE / SHOP
  floors at their old body counts (CACHE d1 4.6, was 4.2). Budget accounting is unchanged
  (COMBAT spends 94-96% of its cap, as before); the synergy of a mixed group is NOT priced in
  TP — the gates judge it. Banded by R-DENSITY's variety half (BalanceSchema.densityVariety
  Results: group cap, brood, mix, body share, COMBAT mono share <= 10%, COMBAT / ELITE mean
  distinct archetypes >= 2.3) and the generator sweep's "room variety (V7)"; asserted by
  BalanceAuditTest.encounterGroupsMixArchetypesAndBehaviours. Measured after (planner, 300
  seeds): 0% of floors with a 4-same group, mono 0-3%, 2.35-2.79 distinct archetypes a group.
  TUNING (balanceSim, the idea's order): the first run read mixed rooms as harder (TACTICAL-NO-
  RELIC mean depth 3.14 -> 2.98, NAIVE median 2 -> 1; SR3 / SR4 relic probes missed). Step 1
  shape weights RABBLE 3.5 / SKIRMISH 1.5 / BATTERY 1.5; step 2 GROUP_MIXED_BEHAVIOUR_MIN_SIZE
  3 -> 4: green, NAIVE median depth 2 again. ENCOUNTER_BUDGET_FILL_TARGET_FRACTION 0.90 was
  tried and REJECTED: R-MYSTERY-EV (1.32) and TWIST T3 are priced off it.
  SIDE EFFECTS: salvage_hook was re-composed (docs/relic-system.txt); GroupBarrelPlacer's
  eligibility now applies the same passage guard as its placement (a room every tile of which
  would plug a passage no longer counts toward H14's 40%).
  OVERRIDE RECORD (mixed-encounter-groups): "PACK 3-5 chaff of ONE type" and "every chaff slot
  fields at least GROUP_CHAFF_SLOT_MIN of ONE archetype" -> chaff PAIRS of DIFFERENT archetypes
  (GROUP_CHAFF_SLOT_MIN 2 is now the pair size; "chaff never spawns alone" still holds, its test
  unmodified); the top-up "grows a group's same-type chaff slot" -> adds a different archetype.

R-SCARCITY-DEPTH (RuleKind.SCARCITY_DEPTH) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 2 (CP3c)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: S in [0.75, 0.95] at every depth on the model floor's dice curve is
  REPLACED by R-SUPPLY's generator sweep at depths {1,5,10,15,20,25}: each floor's
  ammo is ammoRatio x its own roster eHP, priced per unit at the on-curve hit growth
  (GameMath.supplyDamagePerUnitAtDepth), so the box count is depth-stable by
  construction. AMMO_SUPPLY_REGION_MULTIPLIER is DELETED (CP6: the route ledger is
  re-derived from the node specs, so nothing reads a region supply trim).

R-HEALDRAIN-DEPTH (RuleKind.HEALDRAIN_DEPTH) — RETIRED by balance-overhaul order 2 (CP3c)
  OVERRIDE RECORD: the 5-15% net-drain band on the model floor is REPLACED by R-SUPPLY's
  heal floor + tracking (the planned heal value = modelled incoming x (1 - the node's
  drainTarget), S3/S4) and, played, by the sim band S-SUPPLY (TACTICAL HP fraction at
  COMBAT exit in [0.55, 0.80], CP7). HEAL_SUPPLY_REGION_MULTIPLIER is DELETED (CP6).

R-CREDITS v2 (RuleKind.CREDITS) — the shop is a place to SPEND (balance-overhaul order 6, F12)
  OVERRIDE RECORD (CP5): the order-3 rule "income per region / one weapon-class buy (12 PP) + 1.5 small
  buys (3 PP) in [0.9, 1.4]" is REPLACED (CREDIT_INCOME_RATIO_*, SHOP_EXPECTED_*_BUYS_PER_REGION deleted).
  Owner D4: "shops everywhere are too expensive". For every region A..E:
    income(d)  = killBounty(d) + chipIncome   (BalanceSchema.combatFloorCreditIncome; bounties linear in d)
    bundle(d)  = LEVEL UP rung price(d) + 1.5 consumables @ SHOP_SMALL_BUY_POWER_POINTS (3 PP)
                 + the region's TIER STEP (to WEAPON_DROP_TIER_MAX_BY_REGION, priced on its ability-PP
                 budget) / REGION_BAND_SIZE                  (GameMath.neededBundlePricePerFloor)
    mean income(d) / mean bundle(d) over the region's floors in [CREDITS_V2_RATIO_MIN 1.10, MAX 1.40]
  — a SURPLUS: an on-curve player affords the ladder rung, the consumables and the region's tier step,
  with change. RE-FIT: SHOP_CREDITS_PER_POWER_POINT 36 -> 15.5 and SHOP_DEPTH_PRICE_SCALE 0.10 -> 0.0
  (income climbs ~5%/floor while the bundle's value climbs by region tier, so any depth ramp on top
  starved the deep regions; measured A 1.37 / B 1.33 / C 1.26 / D 1.15 / E 1.33). PRICING FROM POWER,
  NOT VIBES still holds: price = valuePP * SHOP_CREDITS_PER_POWER_POINT * depthFactor(d)
  (GameMath.shopPrice), zero hand-set base prices.
  MACHINES (F13, OVERRIDES SHOP_MIN_PER_FLOOR 1 / SHOP_MAX_PER_FLOOR 2 / SHOP_SECOND_MACHINE_CHANCE 0.40):
  exactly SHOP_MACHINES_PER_FLOOR (1) Fabricator on every floor; a SHOP node's floor has
  SHOP_NODE_MACHINE_COUNT (2, upgrade/supply biased) at SHOP_NODE_PRICE_MULTIPLIER (0.80) — through
  ShopContext's price multiplier, stacking with the build's Haggler. The LEVEL UP rung stays guaranteed.
  RIPPLE (CP5, same checkpoint): a credit now buys ~2.3x the power in the route model, so credit-light
  rows fell behind COMBAT — NODE_SUPPLY_CACHE_CREDITS 1.20 -> 1.50 (R-CALM-COST cache 0.40 at d25) and
  AFFIX_OVERCLOCKED_VAULT_AMMO_BOXES 1 -> 2 (R-RISK-PREMIUM 0.999 at d16). The MYSTERY table (0.82x
  COMBAT at d25) carries a waiver that expires at CP6, which re-derives R-MYSTERY-EV (F15).
  S-SHOP (sim, F14; RuleKind.SIM_SHOP): TACTICAL buys >= 1.5 offers per Fabricator visit (round-pending
  waiver since order 7 CP1: measured 1.00 with navigation fixed; was 0.64 while runs stalled) and dies holding <= 1.5
  floors of income (ENFORCED; measured 0.13 — it spends everything it collects).

R-BOSS-* (RuleKind.BOSS_GATE / BOSS_FAIR / BOSS_VERB_CAP / BOSS_REWARD / BOSS_AMMO) — bosses as BUILD CHECKS (order 6)
  RE-DERIVED (balance-overhaul order 1): BossBalance reads THE one expected-player model —
  boss HP from GameMath.expectedPlayerAtDepth(d).damagePerTurn (rounded UP, so the fair
  fight is never a fraction of a turn short), boss DPT and every verb cap from that
  player's eHP (was the fixed 205), the GATE from the START weapon on the ladder (L1
  COMMON at the boss's threat level) with max heals as a share of that player's eHP, and
  the credit reward from the fight's consumption read in depth-1 ammo terms at the
  depth's shop price level (GameMath.shopDepthPriceFactor) — so a deep boss pays a deep
  shop's prices, not the ladder-inflated HP number. BOSS_EXPECTED_OFFENCE_BUDGET_FRACTION
  and BOSS_EXPECTED_LEVELS_PER_DEPTH no longer feed the derivation. The text below is the
  order-6 original; its worked numbers pre-date the ladder (see the living table).
  R-REGION (RuleKind.REGION_DANGER; re-shaped by balance-overhaul order 6, CP4 — F7, OVERRIDE): the region
  TP dial REGION_TP_BUDGET_MULTIPLIER = {A 0.85, B 1.00, C 1.25, D 1.20, E 1.30} — one entry per region of the
  finite run (was {0.90, 1.00, 1.15, 1.25}); every entry in [0.8, 1.5] (was [0.8, 1.3]); the shape is
  C >= B >= A and E >= D (REPLACES "monotonic non-decreasing A<=B<=C<=D": the peak the owner asked for sits at
  REACTOR DEPTHS, floors 11-15, and THE BREACH dips as the build comes online); C/D/E out-dial A by >= 0.1; the
  ladder holds in every lane. ELITE node weights by region (RouteMapConstants) A 0.6 / B 1.0 / C 1.6 / D 1.4 /
  E 1.6. Consequences absorbed in the same checkpoint: BUILD_PYRO_FIRE_TURNS_PER_FLOOR 5 -> 5.5 (more bodies at
  d15 diluted FIREPROOF r1 to +9.8%), the R-CALM-COST and R-TRAJECTORY XP-pace overrides above, the R-ROUTE-*
  audit horizon 15 -> RUN_FINAL_DEPTH (A5), the LevelGeneratorSnapshotTest re-baseline.
  OVERRIDE RECORD (balance-overhaul order 6, CP3 — THE BOSS TABLE, F3/F9): the modulo boss
  rotation (BossBalance.archetypeForDepth) and the per-boss OVERSEER/CORRUPTOR/HELL_BARON_DEPTH
  (5/10/15) + *_TARGET_FIGHT_TURNS (22/28/34) constants are DELETED. route/BossTable holds one
  register() row per boss floor of the finite run — depth, archetype, target fight turns, survival
  ratio — from BalanceConfig SECTION 25: 5 OVERSEER 22, 10 CORRUPTOR 26, 15 HELL_BARON 30,
  20 OVERSEER 32, 25 CORRUPTOR 36, survival 0.50 each (the old cycle made explicit, so the Core-
  ending boss is unchanged). Every R-BOSS-* rule now runs on EVERY row (verb caps on every row the
  verb's archetype fights). R-BOSS-FAIR v2: fight length in target +/- BOSS_FIGHT_TURNS_TOLERANCE
  (25%) — was [target, 1.5*target]; the 1.5x cap survives only as the soft-enrage trigger — plus
  each row's target inside the act band [18, 40] and its survival ratio inside [0.4, 0.7].
  R-SUPPLY S6 carrier share: a boss arena (spec.bossArenaAmmo) is out of the rule's domain — it has no
  carrier roster by design; the 36-turn depth-25 arena was the first to cross the emit threshold.
  A boss's HP, DPT, every verb's damage, XP and credit reward are DERIVED at spawn by
  BossBalance.statsForDepth from its BossTable row (target fight turns + survival ratio) through
  the GameMath BOSS BALANCE RULESET — there are ZERO flat boss HP/damage/reward constants left.
  The rules assert the derivation upholds the contract on every BossTable row (5, 10, 15, 20, 25):
    R-BOSS-GATE     with the DEPTH-1 starting loadout (reference DPT), turnsToKill(boss)
                    >= BOSS_GATE_MIN_DAMAGE_MARGIN (2.0) x survivableTurns computed WITH
                    the maximum modelled heal supply (25% eHP). The start weapon cannot
                    win — a perfect-play hoarder dies at < half the damage needed. The
                    margin = (expectedDPT/refDPT)/survivalRatio, so it RISES with depth
                    (Overseer 2.08x is the tightest; Corruptor 2.56x; Hell Baron 3.04x).
    R-BOSS-FAIR     (v2, order 6) the EXPECTED loadout gets a fight in target +/- 25% turns
                    with a survival ratio in [0.4, 0.7] (the row's dial, 0.50 by construction).
    R-BOSS-VERB-CAP every DERIVED verb: un-telegraphed <= 25% eHP; TELEGRAPHED <= 50% eHP
                    (BOSS_TELEGRAPHED_HEAVY_MAX_FRACTION). OVERRIDDEN by balance-overhaul
                    order 4 (CP1): was every verb <= 35% (BOSS_HARD_SINGLE_HIT_FRACTION,
                    kept as a constant, no longer the telegraphed cap).
    R-BOSS-REWARD   credit reward >= modelled fight consumption (ammo + heal value in shop
                    units) x BOSS_REWARD_RISK_PREMIUM (1.3) — a boss floor is a net-positive
                    payday, never a loss for progressing. XP flows from boss Threat Points.
    R-BOSS-AMMO     the fight's ammo demand (eHP) is coverable by a full reserve at that
                    depth + the arena ammo budget (BOSS_ARENA_AMMO_BUDGET_FRACTION), so the
                    check tests your BUILD, not whether you arrived with full pockets.
  WHY: knowledge-doc SECTION 9 problem 4 — the three bosses were flat, eyeballed HP priced
  against the depth-1 shotgun, which is exactly why a level-1 weapon could beat them and
  every upstream progression system became optional. Derivation makes "beat the boss with
  the starting weapon" arithmetically impossible, verified by the audit. Soft ENRAGE
  (BOSS_ENRAGE_DPT_RAMP_PER_TURN, +8%/turn past 1.5*target, telegraphed "FURIOUS") enforces
  the R2 no-sponge ceiling by escalation, not a fail timer. The route generator guarantees a
  reachable SHOP or CACHE in the layer before every boss (the "sharpen your knife" beat),
  proven over 100 seeds (RouteMapGeneratorTest). Boss AI CHOREOGRAPHY is untouched — order 6
  prices the fight, not the tactic brain.

R-ROUTE-* (RuleKind.ROUTE_PRICED / RISK_PREMIUM / CALM_COST / MYSTERY_EV / PIPS_DERIVED / HONEST_SAFE /
           TRAJECTORY / ROUTE_GUARANTEES) — ROUTE ECONOMICS: the branching MAP joins the contract (order 7)
  Orders 1-6 balance FLOORS, but the player never plays "a floor" — they play a JOURNEY through the
  route map's DAG (COMBAT / ELITE / CACHE / REST / SHOP / MYSTERY / EVENT), and that macro layer is
  where run difficulty is actually decided. Order 7 prices it. Every node type, ELITE affix and MYSTERY
  outcome carries a NodeEconomics row (route/RouteEconomics — the map's twin of the weapon/enemy/heal
  registries); route/RouteEconomicsModel folds a row into one comparable EV through the SAME order-3/4
  models (supply/demand, heal economy, credit income, derived XP) in the shared POWER POINT currency:
      threatCost    = regionScaledFloorBudget(depth) * budgetScale * affixMultiplier   (GameMath.nodeThreatCost)
      resourceDelta = (supply - demand)/ammoPP + (heal - incoming)/healPP + credits/creditPP
      progress      = XP-as-levels * EXPECTED_PERK_POWER_PP_PER_PICK + upgradeOpportunity * significant-buy PP
      EV            = resourceDelta + PERMANENT_POWER_WEIGHT * progress
                      - RISK_PP_PER_STANDARD_FLOOR * threatRatio                (GameMath.nodeExpectedValue)
  PERMANENT_POWER_WEIGHT (2.6) exists because a level or a gun applies to EVERY remaining floor while an
  ammo box is spent once; without it the model says fighting never pays, which is exactly how the
  un-priced map let a calm-chaining route out-earn a fighting one. The risk charge rides the threat
  RATIO, not raw Threat Points, so it is depth-stable.
    R-ROUTE-PRICED     COVERAGE for the map: a node type, affix or mystery outcome with NO ledger row
                       fails the build — the same anti-"shipped unpriced" rule order 5 applied to enemies.
    R-RISK-PREMIUM     (reward premium)/(threat premium) in [1.0, 1.2] for ELITE and EVERY affixed
                       variant: danger pays, slightly better than fair, never free and never a sucker
                       bet. SWARM/OVERCLOCKED raise threatCost, so they now carry VAULT PREMIUMS
                       (AFFIX_*_VAULT_AMMO_BOXES) that price up with them.
    R-TWIST            (NEW, floor-twists CP3; RuleKind.TWIST) every COMBAT FLOOR TWIST priced as COMBAT
                       with its Kind.TWIST NodeEconomics row folded on, against plain COMBAT, at every
                       depth from the twist's minDepth to RUN_FINAL_DEPTH, worst depth reported. T1 a
                       twist with budgetScale > 1 (SALVAGE, HUNTED): reward premium / threat premium in
                       R-RISK-PREMIUM's own band [1.0, 1.2] — measured 1.04 / 1.02 (d25). T2 a twist with
                       budgetScale == 1 (BLACKOUT): total EV within +-TWIST_NEUTRAL_EV_TOLERANCE (0.10) of
                       plain COMBAT — measured 0.00. T3 HUNTED honesty: the IRON_STALKER's real Threat-Point
                       price at the depth / the COMBAT floor's TP budget <= TWIST_HUNTED_THREAT_SHARE (0.78)
                       — measured 0.765 at d4 (it is a MINI_ELITE). Twist rows are a SEPARATE Kind so
                       R-RISK-PREMIUM's AFFIX loop never folds a twist onto ELITE; R-ROUTE-PRICED covers
                       them through affixes().ids(); R-TRAJECTORY folds them with no code (the walk already
                       folds affixRowFor(node)). Asserted by BalanceAuditTest.floorTwistsArePricedAgainstPlainCombat.
                       TWO MODEL GAPS, both deliberate: (1) the ledger prices HUNTED by SCALING THE ROSTER,
                       which also scales planned supply/heal the hunter does not bring (~0.90 premium with
                       zero bounty), partly offset by pricing the hunter as ALWAYS arriving (a fast player
                       gets a cheaper floor, which is the point) — hence the 10-box bounty; (2) the sim does
                       not navigate by the mini-map, so BLACKOUT's information loss is invisible to it and
                       it reads only the wake-perception change — which is why BLACKOUT is priced neutral
                       (T2) and its light/minimap half is a playtest feel question.
    R-CALM-COST        a calm node's EV sits [10%, 40%] BELOW a standard combat node's (OVERRIDE, balance-
                       overhaul order 6 CP4: MAX 0.35 -> 0.40 — the audit horizon is now 1..25 and the F7
                       dial puts REACTOR DEPTHS at 1.25 and THE CORE at 1.30, so combat out-grows calm
                       further at the peak; measured cache 0.36 / shop 0.37 at d21) — the route doc's
                       "costs loot/tempo, never the clock" invariant as a number. The band's top edge is
                       0.35 rather than the idea's 0.30 because the discount necessarily WIDENS with
                       depth (a combat floor's own reward grows with the gear/ability curves while a
                       sanctuary payoff is capped by the player's own maxima) and ONE band must cover
                       depths 1..15. This rule is what RE-PRICED the order-8/9 hand-set payoffs: cache
                       supply down, med-bay heal + stock up, elite vault down, event payout up.
    R-MYSTERY-EV v2    (OVERRIDE, balance-overhaul order 6 CP6, F15 — was "within ±15% of a standard combat
                       node"): every outcome pays a hidden reward in LADDER CURRENCIES (MysteryOutcome.reward,
                       paid on leaving the sector), and at every depth 1..25 the weighted table's EV is in
                       [1.0, 1.0 + ROUTE_MYSTERY_EV_PREMIUM_MAX 0.30] x a standard combat node's (a gamble
                       worth taking, never the strictly-better pick; measured 1.00..1.29), its outcomes'
                       weighted std is >= ROUTE_MYSTERY_MIN_SPREAD_FRACTION 0.25 of the combat EV ("higher
                       variance"; measured >= 0.58), and the worst outcome's expected resource loss stays
                       bounded by one floor's maximum modelled drain x 1.5 — "bad but survivable".
    R-EVENT-VALUE      (NEW, balance-overhaul order 6 CP6, F15; RuleKind.EVENT_VALUE) every registered EVENT
                       offers a choice whose ladder-currency value (RouteEconomicsModel.bestChoicePrice:
                       resources + 2.6 x progress, a gamble at its expectation) is >= EVENT_BEST_CHOICE_MIN_
                       SUPPLY_RATIO 1.0 x one standard COMBAT floor's SUPPLY (ammo + heal + credits PP) at
                       every depth 1..25 (measured 1.08..1.40). LADDER CURRENCIES (EventEffect): a weapon
                       level (ROUTE_WEAPON_LEVEL_POWER_POINTS = one perk pick, 12 PP of progress), a perk rank
                       (SHOP_PERK_RANK_POWER_POINTS), heal / armour fractions of the depth-1 maxima, credits
                       in floors of a COMBAT floor's income at the depth; costs: HP fraction, credits, a hotter
                       next floor (bonus x ROUTE_RISK_POWER_POINTS_PER_STANDARD_FLOOR). The EVENT ledger row
                       is priced from the catalog (weighted mean of each event's best choice), and the EVENT
                       node leaves R-CALM-COST's domain for this rule (OVERRIDE; its "event EV discount"
                       waiver is RETIRED). The EVENT ammo boxes / XP / ROUTE_EVENT_EXPECTED_CHOICE_SHARE are
                       deleted; BUILD_* untouched. RouteRegistries.events() now latch-registers like the
                       other audited registries (the audit prices the catalog without a full bootstrap).
                       Test UPDATED (same checkpoint): safestAndDeadliestRoutesSpanARealDifficultyRange now
                       proves the range on banked HEALTH (net drain) + XP pace at horizon 25 — ammo scarcity
                       has been pinned to demand by the planner since order 2 (0.95 vs 0.93 before this
                       order) and F15 removed the last route-dependent ammo grant.
    R-PIPS-DERIVED     DangerTier is DERIVED from the priced threat ratio, never hand-assigned
                       (GameMath.derivedDangerTierIndex: forced -> SET_PIECE, hidden outcome table ->
                       GAMBLE, else CALM <= 0.60x <= STANDARD <= 1.25x < DANGER), and must equal what the
                       overlay displays. The pips are the player's ONLY route information: if they drift
                       from the price the map LIES and informed choice collapses.
    R-HONEST-SAFE      CACHE/REST face <= 0.30x a combat floor's Threat Points (the HONEST-SAFE-NODE
                       design rule as an enforced bound — a "med-bay ambush" must be a MYSTERY wearing
                       its own face), and the MYSTERY scan tones partition consistently with their
                       prices: REWARD-RICH really is the richest bucket by EV, HIGH RISK really is the
                       most dangerous by threat.
    R-TRAJECTORY       the JOURNEY replaces the floor as the audited unit. 100 real maps are generated
                       headlessly and walked under three deterministic policies (SAFEST / DEADLIEST /
                       BALANCED); at every region boundary the cumulative order-3/4 quantities are
                       measured over the nodes ACTUALLY VISITED, each priced by its own row: scarcity S
                       [0.55, 1.70], per-floor net HP drain [-0.85, +0.65], XP pace [0.70, 3.20] (OVERRIDE,
                       balance-overhaul order 6 CP4: MAX 2.95 -> 3.20 — the F7 peak is more bodies on the
                       fight-everything route; DEADLIEST measured 3.07; coupling stays the end-stop), and
                       depth coupling [0.80, 1.65] AT THE LEVEL THE ROUTE ACTUALLY BOUGHT (a route that
                       skips fights stands below the curve — GameMath.levelForCumulativeXp; coupling
                       is GameMath.ladderCouplingAtCharacterLevel since balance-overhaul order 1). RE-FIT
                       RECORD (balance-overhaul order 1): the four UPPER ends moved to the re-measured
                       range + ~5% (SAFEST S 1.60, DEADLIEST drain 0.62 / XP pace 2.78 / coupling 1.58):
                       R8 fights land harder, the XP curve steepened to 1.2495/level, and coupling is now
                       read on the ladder model. The route model now reads AMMO in depth-1 damage terms
                       (divided by the expected hit growth, like HP already was) and CREDITS at the
                       depth's shop price level, and guaranteed heals are fractions (no /damage-curve
                       decay; the REST clinic stock no longer grows with depth). Payoffs re-priced for
                       the box-size cut: REST_STIMS 1 -> 2, EVENT_AMMO_BOXES 5 -> 6, EVENT_XP_LARGE
                       150 -> 200, AFFIX_SWARM_VAULT_AMMO_BOXES 4 -> 9. All three
                       must hold: SAFEST rides the generous edge, DEADLIEST the starved edge, and those
                       ENDS are the game's real difficulty range (printed by BalanceReport).
    R-ROUTE-GUARANTEES no lane strands a run. Enforced at MAP-GEN by a deterministic no-RNG fixpoint
                       post-pass (RouteMapGenerator.repairBandGuarantees) and verified over the same 100
                       seeds: from every node with room ahead of it an UPGRADE-bearing node (ELITE/SHOP)
                       and a CALM node stay reachable inside the region; the layer before every boss
                       offers reachable provisioning (order 6's beat, which is also what covers the
                       band's tail); and every selectable layer offers >= 2 DISTINCT node types (no fake
                       choices). Order 2's "an upgrade per region" pity rule is now a graph property.
  WHY: knowledge-doc problem "the audited game and the played game diverge at the first branch". A
  player who chained caches sailed far above the order-3 supply band; one who never routed calm starved
  below it; ELITE's risk/reward and every affix multiplier were unpriced; and the risk pips were hand-set
  so the map could quietly lie. Full subsystem detail: docs/route-map-system.txt (ROUTE ECONOMICS).

R-SINGLE-DIFFICULTY (RuleKind.SINGLE_DIFFICULTY) — ONE difficulty, locked (order 8)
  DESIGN INVARIANT (owner decision, final): the game has EXACTLY ONE difficulty. There are no
  easy/normal/hard modes and there must never be. Every run starts from the one canonical
  attribute block — BalanceConfig SECTION 7: PLAYER_START_STRENGTH / _AGILITY / _TOUGHNESS /
  _MARKSMANSHIP = 2/2/2/2, which with PLAYER_MAX_HEALTH 130 + PLAYER_MAX_ARMOR 75 IS the
  REFERENCE_PLAYER_EHP = 205 anchor every band in orders 1-7 was derived against.
  Two structural checks (reflection, no gameplay values):
    1. BalanceConfig declares exactly ONE starting block — one PLAYER_START_<ATTRIBUTE>
       constant per Attribute. A second block is how a second mode starts.
    2. No constant, nested type or enum constant in any scanned class carries mode-family
       naming (RECRUIT / NIGHTMARE / ULTRA / DIFFICULTY / STAT_BASE_ / EASY_MODE / HARD_MODE) —
       the same tripwire R-FLAGS uses for live test flags. BalanceAuditTest adds the
